, except that

\mathcal{L}(0)\geq\sum_{i=1}^{p}\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{(\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t)))_{i}^{2}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon+F_{i}(t)/(1-a^{t})}}\mathrm{d}t\geq\sum_{i=1}^{p}\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{(\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t)))_{i}^{2}}{\sqrt{M_{i}/(1-a^{1})+\varepsilon}}\mathrm{d}t.

The proof is completed.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5
for Adam flow.

We only need to prove Lebesgue integrability of
\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}
.

The proof is the same as proof of Theorem
5
for RMSProp flow, except that

\displaystyle F_{i}(t^{\prime}_{k,j})/(1-a^{t^{\prime}_{k,j}})-F_{i}(t_{k,j})/(1-a^{t_{k,j}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle F_{i}(t^{\prime}_{k,j})/(1-a^{t^{\prime}_{k,j}})-F_{i}(t_{k,j})/(1-a^{t^{\prime}_{k,j}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-a}(F_{i}(t^{\prime}_{k,j})-F_{i}(t_{k,j})).

The proof is completed.
∎

It is worth noting that the current framework of adaptive gradient flow can not cover Adam with a decaying
\varepsilon
or without
\varepsilon
. It will be interesting to see if the framework can be modified to analyze these optimizers, and we leave this as a future work.

B.2
Proof of Theorem
2

B.2.1
Proof of surrogate margin Lemmas: Lemma
1
, Lemma
2
, and Lemma
3

In the beginning, we first prove a basic lemma for normalized margin, i.e., the normalized margin
\gamma
and normalized gradients are upper bounded:

Lemma 17
.

For any
\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}/\{\boldsymbol{0}\}
, the normalized margin
\gamma=\frac{\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L}}
and normalized gradients
\|\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L-1}}\|
(
i=1,2,\cdots,p
) are upper bounded universally.

Proof.

By homogeneity of
\tilde{q}_{i}
(
i=1,2,\cdots,p
), only parameters with unit norm needed to be considered. That is,

\displaystyle\left\{\gamma(\boldsymbol{v}):\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}/\{\boldsymbol{0}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\gamma(\boldsymbol{v}):\|\boldsymbol{v}\|=1\right\}.

Since
\tilde{q}_{i}
is continuous and
\{\boldsymbol{v}:\|\boldsymbol{v}\|=1\}
is a compact set, normalized margin is upper bounded.

Normalized gradients
\|\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L-1}}\|
are also bounded following similar routine since
\tilde{q}_{i}
is locally Lipschitz.
∎

We then formally define surrogate norm
\rho(t)
and surrogate margin
\tilde{\gamma}
as follows:

Definition 9

(Surrogate norm and surrogate margin)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}(t)
obey an adaptive gradient flow
\mathcal{F}
which satisfies Assumption
1
, with loss
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
and component learning rate
\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)
. The surrogate margin
\rho(t)
along
\mathcal{F}
is defined as

\rho(t)=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|,

and surrogate margin
\tilde{\gamma}(t)
is defined as

\tilde{\gamma}(t)=\frac{f^{-1}(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))})}{\rho(t)^{L}}.

We can now restate Lemma
1
as follows:

Lemma 18

(Lemma
1
restated)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}(t)
obey an adaptive gradient flow
\mathcal{F}
which satisfies Assumption
1
, with loss
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
and component learning rate
\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)
. Then we have
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\rho(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}=1
. Furthermore, if further
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=\infty
, we have
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\gamma(t)}{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}=1

Proof.

By the definition of approximate norm
\rho(t)=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|
and
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)=\mathbf{1}_{p}
, we have that

1=\underline{\lim}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\min_{i}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)\leq\underline{\lim}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\rho(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\leq\overline{\lim}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\rho(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\leq\overline{\lim}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\max_{i}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)=1,

which leads to
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\rho(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}=1
.

By the definition of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})
, we have

e^{-f(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}))}\leq\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-f(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}))}\leq Ne^{-f(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}))}.

Rearranging the above equation, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})\geq g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\right)\geq g(f(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}))-\log N)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g(f(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})))-\log Ng^{\prime}(\xi)=\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})-\log Ng^{\prime}(\xi),

where
\xi\in(f(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}))-\log N,f(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})))
.

Therefore, the surrogate margin can be bounded as

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\log Ng^{\prime}(\xi)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}{\rho(t)^{L}}=\frac{\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\log Ng^{\prime}(\xi)}{\rho(t)^{L}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{\gamma}(t)\leq\frac{\tilde{q}_{\min}}{\rho(t)^{L}}=\gamma(t)\left(\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}{\rho(t)}\right)^{L}.

(11)

By the assumption that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}=0
, there exists a large enough time
\tilde{t}
, such that, any time
t\geq\tilde{t}
,
g(\xi)>0
. The left side of the above equation
11
can be further rearranged as

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\log Ng^{\prime}(\xi)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}{\rho(t)^{L}}=

\displaystyle\left(\gamma(t)-\frac{\log Ng^{\prime}(\xi)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\right)\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}{\rho(t)^{L}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\gamma(t)-\frac{\log Ng^{\prime}(\xi)g(\xi)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}g(\xi)}\right)\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}{\rho(t)^{L}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(\gamma(t)-\frac{\log Ng^{\prime}(\xi)\gamma(t)}{g(\xi)}\right)\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}{\rho(t)^{L}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\gamma(t)-\frac{\log N\gamma(t)}{g(\xi)f^{\prime}(g(\xi))}\right)\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}{\rho(t)^{L}}.

By the assumption that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=0
,
\underline{\lim}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\xi\geq\underline{\lim}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}-\log N=\infty
, which further indicates
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}g(\xi)f^{\prime}(g(\xi))=\infty
by the third item of Proposition
1
.
The proof is completed by taking
t
to infinity of eq. (
11
).

∎

By Lemmas
17
and
18
, we have that if
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)\rightarrow 0
,
\tilde{\gamma}(t)
is upper bounded. We then lower bound
\tilde{\gamma}(t)
by proving Lemma
2
. As a warm-up, we first calculate the derivative of
\rho^{2}
.

Lemma 19
.

The derivative of
\rho^{2}
is as follows:

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho(t)^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t}=L\nu(t)+\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\rangle,

where
\nu(t)
is defined as
\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
. Furthermore, we have that
\nu(t)>\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
.

Proof.

By taking derivative directly, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho(t)^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t),\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}+\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t),\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\rangle+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}L\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle,

where eq.
(*)
comes from Homogeneity Assumption
1
. I.

Furthermore, since
\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\geq\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\geq g(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))})
for all
i\in[N]
and
f^{\prime}(x)x
keeps increasing on
(0,\infty)
by Proposition
1
,

f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\geq f^{\prime}\left(g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right)\cdot g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)=\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}=\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)).

The proof is completed.
∎

With the estimation of
\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t}
above, we come to the proof of Lemma
2
.

Proof of Lemma
2
.

We first construct time
t_{1}
as follows: by properties of
\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)
in Definition
1
, there exists some large enough time
t_{1}>t_{0}
, such that for any
t>t_{1}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)\right)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{2L},\frac{1}{4}\right\},

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)\right)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{-}\geq-\min\left\{\frac{1}{2L},\frac{1}{4}\right\},

and

\frac{1}{2}\leq\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{3}{2}.

Taking
logarithmic derivative to
\tilde{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\log\tilde{\gamma}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\log\left(g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right)-L\log\rho(t)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\cdot\left(-\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)-L^{2}\cdot\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho(t)^{2}}-\frac{L\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\rangle}{\rho(t)^{2}}.

Let
A=\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\cdot\left(-\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)-L^{2}\cdot\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho(t)^{2}}
and
B=\frac{L\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\rangle}{\rho(t)^{2}}
. We then have

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\nu(t)}\cdot\left(-\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)-L^{2}\cdot\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho(t)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\nu(t)}\cdot\left(-\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}-\frac{L^{2}\nu(t)^{2}}{\rho(t)^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\nu(t)}\left(\left\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-1}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\geq}0,

where inequality
(*)
comes from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

As for
B
, we have that

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=-\frac{L\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\rangle}{\rho^{2}}

\displaystyle=-L\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{p}v_{i}^{2}(t)\beta_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\frac{\mathrm{d}\beta_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}}{\sum_{i=1}^{p}v_{i}^{2}(t)\beta_{i}^{-1}(t)}

\displaystyle\geq-L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}.

Combining the estimation of
A
and
B
, we then have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\log\tilde{\gamma}(t)\geq A+B\geq-L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+},

(12)

and integrating both sides leads to

\log\tilde{\gamma}(t)-\log\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})\geq-\frac{1}{2}.

The proof is completed.

∎

By the proof of Lemma
2
, we can then prove convergence of surrogate margin
\tilde{\gamma}
.

Proof of Lemma
3
.

By eq. (
12
),
\log\tilde{\gamma}(t)+\int_{t_{1}}^{t}L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\right)_{+}\mathrm{d}\tau
is non-decreasing. Furthermore, since
g(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))})\leq\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
, we have that

\overline{\lim}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\gamma}(t)\leq\overline{\lim}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\rho(t)^{L}}=\overline{\lim}_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}},

which by Lemma
17
, the last term is bounded.

Therefore,
\log\tilde{\gamma}(t)+\int_{t_{1}}^{t}L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}\mathrm{d}\tau
is upper bounded, which further indicates that it converges due to its monotony. The proof is completed by the convergence of
\int_{t_{1}}^{t}L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\right)_{+}\mathrm{d}\tau
.

∎

B.2.2
Convergence of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
and
\rho
: Proof of Lemma
4

Here we restate the complete Lemma
4
.

Lemma 20
.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}
obey an adaptive gradient flow which satisfies Assumption
1
. Let
t_{1}
be constructed as Lemma
2
. Then, for any
t\geq t_{1}
, define

G(t)=\int_{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1})}}^{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}}\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log x\right)^{2}}{g\left(\log x\right)^{2-2/L}}\cdot dx.

Then, for any
t\geq t_{1}
, the following inequality holds:

G(t)-G(t_{1})\geq\int_{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1})}}^{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}}\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log x\right)^{2}}{g\left(\log x\right)^{2-2/L}}\cdot dx\geq(t-t_{1})e^{-\frac{1}{L}}L^{2}\tilde{\gamma}\left(t_{1}\right)^{2/L}.

Consequently,
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}=0
and
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\rho=\infty
.

Proof.

-\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}=\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\rangle^{2}=L^{2}\cdot\frac{\nu(t)^{2}}{\rho(t)^{2}}.

Furthermore, we have that

\displaystyle L^{2}\frac{\nu(t)^{2}}{\rho(t)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq L^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\right)^{2/L}

\displaystyle\geq L^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{e^{-1/2}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})}{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\right)^{2/L}.

By simple calculation, we have that

\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)^{2}}{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)^{2-2/L}}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\geq e^{-\frac{1}{L}}L^{2}\tilde{\gamma}\left(t_{1}\right)^{2/L}.

Taking integration to both sides, we have

\int_{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1})}}^{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}}\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log x\right)^{2}}{g\left(\log x\right)^{2-2/L}}\cdot dx\geq(t-t_{1})e^{-\frac{1}{L}}L^{2}\tilde{\gamma}\left(t_{1}\right)^{2/L}.

Since
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}(t-t_{1})e^{-\frac{1}{L}}L^{2}\tilde{\gamma}\left(t_{1}\right)^{2/L}=\infty
, we have that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}=\infty
.

The proof is completed.
∎

B.2.3
Verification of KKT Condition

In Lemma
5
, we omit the construction of coefficients
\lambda_{i}
to highlight the key factors of coefficients
(\mathcal{O}(1-\langle\hat{v}(t),\widehat{-\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\rangle),\mathcal{O}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right))
. We restate Lemma
5
and provide the detailed construction as follows:

Lemma 21

(Lemma
5
restated)
.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}
obey adaptive gradient flow
\mathcal{F}
with empirical loss
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
satisfying Assumption
1
. Let time
t_{1}
be constructed as Lemma
2
. Then, define coefficients in Definition
2
as
\lambda_{i}(t)=\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{1-2/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|\cdot e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)/\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|_{2}
. Then, for any time
t\geq t_{1}
,
\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)=\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{-\frac{1}{L}}\boldsymbol{v}(t)
is an
(\varepsilon(t),\delta(t))
KKT point of
L^{2}
max-margin problem
(P)
defined in Theorem
2
, where
\varepsilon(t)
,
\delta(t)
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\varepsilon(t)

\displaystyle=8e^{\frac{1}{L}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})^{2/L}}(1-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)))

\displaystyle\delta(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{2e^{-1+\frac{1}{L}}KN}{L}K^{\log_{2}\left(2^{L+1}e^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}/\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})\right)}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})^{-2/L}\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}},

where
\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t))
is defined as the cosine of angle between
\boldsymbol{v}(t)
and
-\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
, i.e.,

\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t))=\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t),-\widehat{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right\rangle.

Proof.

We verify the definition of approximate KKT point directly.

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}(t)\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}\left(\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{-1/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)-\frac{\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{-1/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)}{\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{-2/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)+\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|_{2}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{-2/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}\left(2-2\left\langle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t),\widehat{-\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2g\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right)^{-2/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}(1-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)))

\displaystyle\leq 8g\left(\log\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right)^{-2/L}\rho(t)^{2}(1-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)))

\displaystyle=8\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t)^{2/L}}(1-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)))\leq 8\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t)^{2/L}}(1-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)))

\displaystyle\leq 8e^{\frac{1}{L}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})^{2/L}}(1-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t))).

As for
\delta
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}(t))-1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{q}_{\min}^{1-2/L}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|\cdot e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\left(\frac{\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}-1\right)}{\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{-2/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|\cdot e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}{\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|_{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{2K}{L}\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{-2/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}e^{f\left(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}},

where inequality
(*)
is because

\displaystyle\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\geq

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle=\frac{L\nu(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{L}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\geq\frac{L}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\frac{1}{2K}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\cdot\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{L}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\frac{1}{2K}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}.

Bounding
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|
using
\rho
and applying the definition of surrogate margin
\tilde{\gamma}
, we further have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}(t))-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{8K}{L}\tilde{q}_{\min}^{-2/L}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\rho(t)^{2}e^{f\left(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{8K}{L}\tilde{\gamma}(t)^{-2/L}\cdot e^{f\left(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{8e^{\frac{1}{L}}K}{L}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})^{-2/L}\cdot e^{f\left(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}.

Since
f(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))-f(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))=(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))f^{\prime}(\xi_{i}(t))
(
\xi_{i}(t)\in[\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))]
is guaranteed by Mean Value Theorem), we then have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}(t))-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{2e^{\frac{1}{L}}K}{L}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})^{-2/L}\cdot e^{f\left(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{2e^{\frac{1}{L}}K}{L}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})^{-2/L}\cdot e^{(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))f^{\prime}(\xi_{i}(t))}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{2e^{\frac{1}{L}}K}{L}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})^{-2/L}\cdot e^{(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))f^{\prime}(\xi_{i}(t))}K^{\left\lceil\log_{2}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))/\xi_{i}(t)\right)\right\rceil}f^{\prime}\left(\xi_{i}(t)\right)

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{2e^{\frac{1}{L}}K}{L}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})^{-2/L}\cdot e^{(\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))f^{\prime}(\xi_{i}(t))}K^{\log_{2}\left(2e^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}/\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\xi_{i}(t)\right)

\displaystyle\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}

\displaystyle\overset{(**)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{2e^{-1+\frac{1}{L}}KN}{L}K^{\log_{2}\left(2^{L+1}e^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}/\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})\right)}\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})^{-2/L}\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}},

where eq.
(*)
is because

\displaystyle\left\lceil\log_{2}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))/\xi_{i}(t)\right)\right\rceil

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))/\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)+1

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}/\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)+1

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\left(2^{L+1}B_{1}\rho(t)^{L}/\tilde{q}_{\min}\right)\leq\log_{2}\left(2^{L+1}B_{1}/\tilde{\gamma}(t)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\left(2^{L+1}e^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}/\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})\right),

which further leads to

f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\leq K^{\log_{2}\left(2e^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}/\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\xi_{i}(t)\right),

and eq.
(**)
is because
e^{-x}x\leq e^{-1}
.

The proof is completed.
∎

By Lemma
4
, we have proved that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=0
, which leads to
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\delta(t)=0
. As stated in the main text, we only need to bound
\varepsilon(t)
, or equivalently
(1-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)))
.

Before moving forward, we introduce an equivalent proposition of that
(1-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}))
goes to zero.

Lemma 22
.

If there exists a time sequence
\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
, such that,
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}t_{i}=\infty
and
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\bar{\partial\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}}\right\rangle=-1
, then

\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t_{i}))=1.

Proof.

\displaystyle-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}+\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right),\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}+\left(\widehat{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right\rangle+\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)},\left(\widehat{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right),\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right),\left(\widehat{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)\right\rangle.

Furthermore,

\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\widehat{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right\|=2-2\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\left\langle\widehat{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right\rangle=0.

Following the same routine, we have
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\|=0
.

The proof is completed.
∎

Let
\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(t))=-\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right\rangle=\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}}\right\rangle
. Then we only need to bound
1-\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(t))
.

In Section
5.3
, we briefly state the methodology of proving the convergence of
\varepsilon(t)
. We will make it more clear here: In the proof Lemma
2
, we show that sum of the derivative of
g(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)})
and
\frac{1}{\rho(t)^{L}}
with
\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)
fixed can be bounded as

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\log\left(g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)\right)\right)-L\left\langle\partial^{s}_{\boldsymbol{v}}\log\rho(t),\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)}{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\cdot\left(-\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)-L^{2}\cdot\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho(t)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu(t)}\left(\left\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-1}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\rangle^{2}}{\nu(t)}(\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(t))^{-2}-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{L^{2}\nu(t)}{\rho(t)^{2}}(\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(t))^{-2}-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L\left\langle\partial^{s}_{\boldsymbol{v}}\log\rho(t),\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle(\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(t))^{-2}-1).

(13)

Eq. (
13
) indicates that, intuitively,
(\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(t))^{-2}-1)
can be bounded by the division of change of
\tilde{\gamma}
to change of parameter part
\boldsymbol{v}
in
\rho
. For this purpose, we define
\tilde{\rho}
in Section
5.3
to describe the accumulated change of
\boldsymbol{v}
in
\rho
. The following lemma describe the basic property of
\tilde{\rho}
and its relationship with
\rho
.

Lemma 23
.

(1). The derivative of
\tilde{\rho}^{2}
is as follows:

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\tilde{\rho}(t)^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t}=\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle=L\nu(t);

(2).
\rho(t)
satisfies that, for any
t^{2}>t^{1}>t_{1}
,

\rho(t_{2})\geq e^{-\frac{1}{2}}\rho(t_{1});

(3). For any
t>t_{1}
,
\sqrt{1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}}\leq\frac{\rho(t)}{\tilde{\rho}(t)}\leq\sqrt{1+\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}}
.

Proof.

(1). can be directly verified similar to Lemma
19
. As for (2).,
since
\frac{d\log\rho(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\frac{d\rho(t)^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t}}{\rho(t)^{2}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{d\log\rho(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle=\frac{L\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))+\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle}{\rho(t)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle}{\rho(t)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\boldsymbol{v}_{i}(t)^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t)\boldsymbol{v}_{i}(t)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t)\boldsymbol{v}_{i}(t)^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t)\boldsymbol{v}_{i}(t)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{d\log\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t)\boldsymbol{v}_{i}(t)^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t)\boldsymbol{v}_{i}(t)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{d\log\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{-}.

The proof for (2). is completed by integration.

As for (3)., by expanding
\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle
, we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}(t)^{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\langle\boldsymbol{v}(\tau),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau)\rangle\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\boldsymbol{v}_{i}^{2}(\tau)\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(\tau)\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(\tau)\boldsymbol{v}_{i}^{2}(\tau)\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}\boldsymbol{v}_{i}^{2}(\tau)\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}\right)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(\tau_{0})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau_{0})\|^{2}\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}\right)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(\tau_{0})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau_{0})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{4}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2},

where
\tau_{0}
in eq.
(*)
is in
[t_{1},t]
and First Mean Value Theorem guarantees its existence.

The second inequality follows by lower bound
\int_{t_{1}}^{t}\langle\boldsymbol{v}(\tau),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau)\rangle\mathrm{d}\tau
similarly.

The proof is completed.
∎

With Lemma
23
, we integrate the analysis of the change of
\tilde{\gamma}
into the following Lemma.

Lemma 24
.

For any time
t_{3}>t_{2}\geq t_{1}
,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{3}}\left(\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\tau))^{-2}-1\right)\cdot\frac{\mathrm{d}}{d\tau}\log\tilde{\rho}(\tau)d\tau\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L\left(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}\right)}\log\frac{\tilde{\gamma}\left(t_{3}\right)}{\tilde{\gamma}\left(t_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{L}{\left(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}\right)}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}\mathrm{d}t.

Proof.

Recall that

\frac{d\log\tilde{\rho}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}=\frac{L\nu(t)}{\tilde{\rho}(t)^{2}}=\frac{\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-1}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle}{\tilde{\rho}(t)^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\log\tilde{\gamma}(t)\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu(t)}\left(\left\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-1}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}.

We then have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\log\tilde{\gamma}(t)\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu(t)}\left(\left\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-1}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L\frac{\tilde{\rho}(t)^{2}}{L^{2}\nu(t)^{2}}\frac{d\log\tilde{\rho}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\left\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-1}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle L\left(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}\right)\frac{\rho(t)^{2}}{L^{2}\nu(t)^{2}}\frac{d\log\tilde{\rho}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\left\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-1}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\rangle\right.

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left.\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\right\rangle^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L\left(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}\right)\frac{d\log\tilde{\rho}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(t))^{-2}-1)-L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}.

The proof is completed.
∎

Applying the First Mean Value Theorem together with Lemmas
22
and
24
, one can easily obtain the first inequality in Lemma
6
. To make the following proof simpler, we restate Lemma
6
as the following corollary, while using
\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(t))
instead of
\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t))
.

Corollary 1

(First inequality in Lemma
6
, restated)
.

For any time
t_{3}>t_{2}\geq t_{1}
, there exists a time
\xi\in(t_{2},t_{3})
, such that,

\displaystyle\left(\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(\xi))^{-2}-1\right)(\log\tilde{\rho}(t_{2})-\log\tilde{\rho}(t_{1}))\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L\left(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}\right)}\log\frac{\tilde{\gamma}\left(t_{3}\right)}{\tilde{\gamma}\left(t_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{L}{\left(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2}\right)}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}\mathrm{d}t.

We then prove the second inequality in Lemma
6
to complete the proof of Lemma
6
.

Proof of Lemma
6
.

By direct calculation, we have that

\left\|\frac{d\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|=\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{I}-\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)^{\top}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|\leq\frac{2}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-1}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-1}\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|=\left\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[N]}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\left\|\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[N]}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}B_{1}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L-1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[N]}2^{L-1}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}B_{1}\rho(t)^{L-1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in[N]}2^{L-1}e^{-f\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\frac{1}{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)}B_{1}\rho(t)^{L-1}

\displaystyle=2^{L-1}\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho(t)}\frac{\rho(t)^{L}}{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)}B_{1}=2^{L-1}\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho(t)}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}B_{1}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{d\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{L}\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho(t)\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}B_{1}\leq 3\cdot 2^{L-1}\frac{\nu(t)}{\rho(t)^{2}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}B_{1}\leq\frac{3\cdot 2^{L-1}}{(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2})}\frac{\nu(t)}{\tilde{\rho}(t)^{2}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}B_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{3\cdot 2^{L-1}}{(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2})}\frac{B_{1}}{L}\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\tilde{\rho}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}\leq\frac{3\cdot 2^{L-1}}{(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2})}\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}}{L}\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\tilde{\rho}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})}.

The proof is completed.
∎

Applying Lemma
5
and Lemma
6
, we can then prove Theorem
2
.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Let
\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}
be any limit point of series
\{\boldsymbol{v}(t)\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
. We construct a series of approximate KKT point which converges to
\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}
by induction.

Let
t^{1}=t_{1}
. Now suppose
t^{k-1}
has been constructed. By Lemma
3
and that
\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}
is a limit point, there exists
s_{k}>t^{k-1}
such that, for any
t>s_{k}

\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\left(s_{k}\right)-\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{k},\text{ }\frac{2}{L}\log\frac{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}{\tilde{\gamma}(s_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{2k^{3}},\text{ and},L\sum_{i=1}^{p}\int_{s_{m}}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\log\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}\right)_{+}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{1}{2k^{3}}.

Let
s^{\prime}_{k}>s_{k}
satisfy
\log\tilde{\rho}\left(s_{k}^{\prime}\right)=\log\tilde{\rho}\left(s_{k}\right)+\frac{1}{k}
(which is guaranteed as
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\rho=\infty
and Lemma
23
). Therefore, by Corollary
1
, there exists
t^{k}\in\left(s_{k},s_{k}^{\prime}\right)
, such that

\cos(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\left(t^{k}\right))^{-2}-1\leq\frac{1}{k^{2}(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2})}.

(14)

Furthermore,

\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\left(t^{k}\right)-\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\left(t^{k}\right)-\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\left(s_{k}\right)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\left(s_{k}\right)-\overline{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{3\cdot 2^{L-1}}{(1-\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2})}\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}}}{L}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}(t_{1})}\frac{1}{k}+\frac{1}{k}\rightarrow 0.

Therefore,
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t^{k})=\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}
. Furthermore, by eq. (
14
) and Lemma
21
, we have that
\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k})/\tilde{q}_{\min}^{\frac{1}{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k}))
is an (
\varepsilon_{i}
,
\delta_{i}
) KKT point with
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\varepsilon_{i}=\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\delta_{i}=0
. Since

\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k})/\tilde{q}_{\min}^{\frac{1}{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k}))=\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t^{k})/\gamma(t^{k}),

and
\gamma(t^{k})
converges to a positive number, we further have

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k})/\tilde{q}_{\min}^{\frac{1}{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k}))=\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}/\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\gamma(t^{k})^{\frac{1}{L}},

is a KKT point of
(P)
, and along the same direction of
\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}
.

The proof is completed.
∎

B.3
Convergent Direction of AdaGrad, RMSProp and Adam (w/m): proof of Theorems
6
and
7

First of all, we prove Theorem
6
by substitute
\boldsymbol{v}
in Theorem
2
with
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}\odot\boldsymbol{w}
.

Proof of Theorem
6
.

Let
\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}
be any limit point of series
\{\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}(t)\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
. Since
\boldsymbol{v}(t)=\boldsymbol{h}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}\odot\boldsymbol{w}(t)
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{h}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}}
is a limit point of
\{\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\}
. By Theorem
2
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{h}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}}/\tilde{q}_{\min}\left(\widehat{\boldsymbol{h}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}}\right)^{1/L}=\boldsymbol{h}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}/q_{\min}(\bar{\boldsymbol{w}})^{1/L}
is a KKT point of
(\tilde{P})
. Therefore, there exist non-negative reals
\{\lambda_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, such that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{h}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}/q_{\min}(\bar{\boldsymbol{w}})^{1/L}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{h}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}/q_{\min}(\bar{\boldsymbol{w}})^{1/L}),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{h}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty}\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}/q_{\min}(\bar{\boldsymbol{w}})^{1/L})-1)=0.

Applying the relationship between
\tilde{q}_{i}
and
q_{i}
, we then have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{h}^{-1}_{\infty}\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}/q_{\min}(\bar{\boldsymbol{w}})^{1/L}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}\partial^{s}q_{i}(\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}/q_{\min}(\bar{\boldsymbol{w}})^{1/L}),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}(q_{i}(\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}/q_{\min}(\bar{\boldsymbol{w}})^{1/L})-1)=0.

The proof is completed.
∎

Theorem
7
can be obtained in the same way.

Proof of Theorem
7
.

The claim holds since

\boldsymbol{v}^{R}
is just
\boldsymbol{w}
with a positive scaling factor,
\boldsymbol{v}^{R}
and
\boldsymbol{w}
share the same direction.

The proof is completed.

∎

B.4
Convergence Rate of Empirical Loss and Parameter Norm

In the end of this section, we will give a tight bound for the convergence rate of empirical loss and parameter norm, which is derived by estimating
G(x)
in Lemma
20
. These results will further be used in Appendix
C
.

Theorem 10
.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}
obey an adaptive gradient flow
\mathcal{F}
with empirical loss
\tilde{L}
satisfying Assumption
1
. Let
G(x)
be defined as Lemma
20
, i.e.,

G(x)=\int_{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1})}}^{x}\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log z\right)^{2}}{g\left(\log z\right)^{2-2/L}}\cdot dz.

Then
G(x)=\Theta(x(\log x)^{\frac{2}{L}-2})
, and
G^{-1}(x)=\Theta(x(\log x)^{2-\frac{2}{L}})
. Consequently,

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{t\log t^{2-\frac{2}{L}}}\right),\text{ and }\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\log t^{\frac{1}{L}}}\right).

Proof.

For any large enough
x
,

\displaystyle G(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1})}}^{x}\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log z\right)^{2}}{g\left(\log z\right)^{2-2/L}}\cdot dz

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}\Theta\left(\int_{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1})}}^{x}\frac{g\left(\log z\right)^{2/L}}{(\log z)^{2}}\cdot dz\right)

\displaystyle=\Theta\left(\int_{\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1})}}^{x}\frac{1}{(\log z)^{2-\frac{2}{L}}}\cdot dz\right)

\displaystyle=\Theta\left(x(\log x)^{\frac{2}{L}-2}\right),

where eq.
(*)
is due to Proposition
1
.

Since
G(x)
is monotonously increasing, and
\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}G(x)=\infty
, we have
x=\Theta\left(G^{-1}(x)(\log G^{-1}(x))^{\frac{2}{L}-2}\right)
, which further leads to
G^{-1}(x)=\Theta(x(\log x)^{2-\frac{2}{L}})
.

Since
\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}=G^{-1}(\Omega(t))
, we have

\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}=\Omega(t(\log t)^{2-\frac{2}{L}})
, which further leads to
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2-\frac{2}{L}}}\right)
.

On the other hand,

-\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}=\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\left\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)^{1-\frac{1}{L}}\right),

which leads to
\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}=G^{-1}(\mathcal{O}(t))
, and
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=\Omega\left(\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2-\frac{2}{L}}}\right)
. Therefore,
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2-\frac{2}{L}}}\right)

By Lemma
17
,
\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}=\Theta\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)
, which leads to
\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{\log t^{\frac{1}{L}}}\right)
.

The proof is completed.
∎

The convergent behavior of
\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
can be derived immediately by the above Theorem.

Corollary 2
.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}
obey an adaptive gradient flow
\mathcal{F}
with empirical loss
\tilde{L}
satisfying Assumption
1
. Then,
\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|=\Theta\left(\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{1-\frac{1}{L}}}\right)
.

Proof.

Since

\Omega\left(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L-1}\right)=\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle\leq\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|=\mathcal{O}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L-1}\right),

the proof is completed.

∎

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
3

In this section, we will prove that direction of parameters converges, that is,
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}
exists. Concretely, define the length swept by
\frac{\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}
as
\zeta(t)
, i.e.,

\zeta(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(\tau)}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\right\|\mathrm{d}\tau.

We will upper bound
\zeta(t)
in the rest of this section.

To begin with, we first show that if the network is definable, then
\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
in adaptive flow is actually
\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
.

Lemma 25
.

If the neural network
\Phi(\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{x})
is definable with respect to
\boldsymbol{w}
for any
\boldsymbol{x}
, for any
\boldsymbol{v}
satisfying the following adaptive gradient flow

\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}=-\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\text{a.e.}t>0,

with
\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\in\partial\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
. Then
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|=\operatorname{dist}(0,\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))
.

The proof of Lemma
25
follows the same routine as that of Lemma 5.2 in
(
Davis et al. 2020
)
, and we omit it here.

We then define another surrogate margin
\bar{\gamma}
as

\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v})=\frac{g(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})})}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L}}+e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}.

The following Lemma then lower bound the derivative of
\bar{\gamma}
.

Lemma 26
.

For large enough
t
, we have a.e.,

\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|+\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|\right),

and

\frac{\mathrm{d}\zeta(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}=\frac{\left\|(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right))_{\perp}\right\|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|}.

Proof.

To begin with, we calculate the rate of
\boldsymbol{\beta}
converging to
\mathbf{1}_{p}
. For AdaGrad, given a fixed index
i\in[N]
, we have that,

\displaystyle 0\leq\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}(t)-1=

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty})^{-1}_{i}(\boldsymbol{h}_{i}(t)-(\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty})_{i})=\Theta(\boldsymbol{h}_{i}(t)-(\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty})_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(\tau))^{2}d\tau\right)=\Theta\left(\int_{t}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\tau^{2}(\log\tau)^{2-\frac{2}{L}}}d\tau\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2-\frac{2}{L}}}\right).

(15)

Similarly, for RMSProp, given a fixed index
i\in[N]
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon+(1-b)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(1-b)(t-\tau)}(\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\tau))_{i}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau}}=\Theta\left((1-b)\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(1-b)(t-\tau)}(\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\tau))_{i}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta\left((1-b)\int_{0}^{t-\sqrt{t}}e^{-(1-b)(t-\tau)}(\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\tau))_{i}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\right)+\Theta\left((1-b)\int_{t-\sqrt{t}}^{t}e^{-(1-b)(t-\tau)}(\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\tau))_{i}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left((1-b)e^{-(1-b)\sqrt{t}}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\partial^{s}{\mathcal{L}}(\tau))_{i}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\right)+\mathcal{O}\left((1-b)\frac{\sqrt{t}}{(t-\sqrt{t})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{t^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2-\frac{2}{L}}}\right).

We then directly calculate the derivative of
\bar{\gamma}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}=

\displaystyle\langle\partial\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\frac{d\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{dt}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle-g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}-L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)-e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\frac{d\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{dt}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)+e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}},\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle+\left\langle L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t),\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle+\left\langle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\langle g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t),(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\mathbf{1})\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(\diamond)}{\geq}

\displaystyle\left\langle g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle+\left\langle L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t),\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle+\left\langle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\langle g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t),(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\mathbf{1})\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle

where Eq. (
\diamond
) is due to
\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
has the smallest norm among
\partial\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
and the homogeneity of the neural network.

Let

\displaystyle A\overset{\triangle}{=}\left\langle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}},\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle B\overset{\triangle}{=}\left\langle g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t),(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\mathbf{1})\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle.

On the one hand, as
e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\rightarrow 1
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\rightarrow\boldsymbol{1}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
, we have

A=(1+\boldsymbol{o}(1))\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\geq(1+\boldsymbol{o}(1))\|\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle|B|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}},(\mathbf{1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}(t))\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}-\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\|_{\infty}\left\|g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\right\|\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2-\frac{2}{L}}}\right)\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\right)\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\right)\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\right)\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\right)\|\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|^{2},

where the last Inequality is due to Lemma
25
and
\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
having the smallest norm.

On the other hand,

\left\langle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\rangle=\Theta(\|\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}).

Therefore, there exists a large enough
T
, such that, any
t\geq T
,

\displaystyle A+B\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\langle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\rangle,

which further leads to

\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\langle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\rangle+\left\langle\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}},\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\rangle.

We then calculate axial component and radial component of
e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})
,
g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}
, and
\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})
respectively as follows:

\displaystyle\left(e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\right)_{\backslash\backslash}=\langle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}},

\displaystyle\left(e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\right)_{\perp}=e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})-\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}),

\displaystyle\left(g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\right)_{\backslash\backslash}=L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{v})}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}+g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}},

\displaystyle\left(g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\right)_{\perp}=g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})-\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L}},

\displaystyle\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\right)_{\backslash\backslash}=\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}

\displaystyle\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\right)_{\perp}=\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})-\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\langle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\rangle+\left\langle g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t),\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\left(e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\backslash\backslash}+\left(e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\perp},\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\backslash\backslash}+\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\perp}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\langle\left(g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right)_{\backslash\backslash}+\left(g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right)_{\perp},\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\backslash\backslash}+\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\perp}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\left\|\left(e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\backslash\backslash}\right\|\left\|\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\backslash\backslash}\right\|+\left\|\left(e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\perp}\right\|\left\|\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\perp}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\left(g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right)_{\backslash\backslash}\right\|\left\|\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\backslash\backslash}\right\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\left(g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right)_{\perp}\right\|\left\|\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\perp}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))+g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right)_{\backslash\backslash}\right\|\left\|\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\backslash\backslash}\right\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\left(e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))+g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}+L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right)_{\perp}\right\|\left\|\left(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)_{\perp}\right\|,

where eq.
(*)
is because

\displaystyle\langle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle=-e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\langle-\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle<0,

\displaystyle e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}>0,

\displaystyle L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}+g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\left(L\nu(t)g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right)<0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}>0,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle=-\langle-\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle<0.

The proof of the first claim is completed since
\bar{\partial}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v})=-e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})-g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L}}-L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}
.

As for the second claim, we have a.e.,

\frac{\mathrm{d}\zeta}{\mathrm{d}t}=\left\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\right\|=\frac{\left\|(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right))_{\perp}\right\|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|}.

The proof is completed.

∎

The following lemma gives an equivalent proposition of that the curve length
\zeta
is finite.

Lemma 27
.

\zeta
is finite if

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|}dt<\infty.

Proof.

By definition,

\displaystyle\|(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right))_{\perp}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\|^{2}-\|(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right))_{\backslash\backslash}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\|^{2}+\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|^{2}+2\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\|^{2}+\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|^{2}+2\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle^{2}-\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)-\boldsymbol{1}\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle^{2}-2\left\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)-\boldsymbol{1}\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\|^{2}+\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|^{2}+2\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle^{2}-\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)-\boldsymbol{1}\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle^{2}-2\left\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)-\boldsymbol{1}\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\|^{2}+2\left\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\boldsymbol{1}\right)\odot\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\rangle+\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)-\boldsymbol{1}\right)\odot\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|^{2}+2\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\rangle

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle^{2}-\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)-\boldsymbol{1}\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle^{2}-2\left\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle\left\langle\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)-\boldsymbol{1}\right)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(**)}{\leq}
