\displaystyle\|\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\|^{2}-\left\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\rangle^{2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{t^{3}}\right).

where Inequality
(*)
is due to Lemma
25
and the homogeneity of the neural network, and Inequality
(**)
is due to
\|1-\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{t(\log t)^{2-\frac{2}{L}}})
by (eq. (
15
)) and the convergent rate of
\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)
and
\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\zeta(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|}dt+\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{t^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)dt.

The proof is completed.
∎

We then prove

\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|}dt<\infty.

Theorem 11
.

There exists
a,\gamma_{0}>0
and a definable desingularizing function
\Phi
on
[0,a)
, such that, for large enough
t
,

\frac{\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\leq-\frac{\mathrm{d}\Psi(\gamma_{0}-\bar{\gamma}(t))}{\mathrm{d}t}.

Proof.

Since
\frac{d\bar{\gamma}(t)}{dt}\geq 0
, and both
\frac{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}
and
e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}
are upper bounded,
\bar{\gamma}(t)
converges to a limit non-decreasingly. Define
\gamma_{0}=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\bar{\gamma}(t)
. If
\bar{\gamma}(t)=\gamma_{0}
for a finite time
t^{0}
, then
\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\gamma}(t)}{dt}=0
for any
t\geq t^{0}
, which further leads to
\|(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})(t))_{\perp}\|=0
, and the proof is then completed by letting
\Psi(x)=x
. Therefore, we only consider the case where
\bar{\gamma}(t)<\gamma_{0}
for any finite time
t
. For any large enough
t
, we further divide the proof into two cases.

Case I.
\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|\geq\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{\frac{L}{4}}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|
.

Applying Lemma
9
to
\gamma_{0}-\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v})|_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|>1}
, there exists an
a_{1}>0
and a definable desingularizing function
\Psi_{1}
, such that if
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|>1
,
\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v})>\gamma_{0}-a_{1}
, and

\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v})\right\|\geq\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{\frac{L}{4}}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v})\right\|,

then

\Psi^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma_{0}-\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}))\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\|\bar{\partial}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\geq 1.

Since
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\bar{\gamma}(t)=\gamma_{0}
, and
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|=\infty
, there exists a large enough time
T_{1}
, such that, for every
t\geq T_{1}
,
\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|>1
, and
\bar{\gamma}(t)>\gamma_{0}-a_{1}
.

Therefore, for any
t\geq T_{1}
which satisfies
\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|\geq\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{\frac{L}{4}}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|
, we have

\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|\geq\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|,

which further indicates

\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bar{\partial}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|.

Furthermore, by Lemma
26
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\gamma}(t)}{dt}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|\left\|\bar{\partial}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|\frac{\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\Psi^{\prime}_{1}(\gamma_{0}-\bar{\gamma}(t))}\frac{\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}.

Case II.
\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|<\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{\frac{L}{4}}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|
.

Applying Lemma
10
to
\gamma_{0}-\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v})|_{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|>1}
, we have that there exists an
a_{2}>0
and a desingularizing function on
[0,a_{2})
, such that if
\boldsymbol{v}>1
, and
\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v})>\gamma_{0}-a_{2}
, then

\max\left\{1,\frac{4}{L}\right\}\Psi_{2}^{\prime}(\gamma_{0}-\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}))\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{\frac{L}{2}+1}\|\bar{\partial}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v})\|\geq 1.

Similar to Case I., there exists a large enough time
T_{2}
and constants
C_{1}
, such that, for every
t\geq T_{1}
,
\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|>1
,
\bar{\gamma}(t)>\gamma_{0}-a_{2}
,
C_{1}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}\leq g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)
, and
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\leq\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{-2L}
.

Therefore,

\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right\|=g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\frac{L\nu(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\geq\frac{Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\geq LC_{1}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L-1},

(16)

and

\displaystyle\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)+g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)+e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-L\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}-g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}-e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\frac{Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}+\|\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}}+B_{1}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L+1}},

(17)

where inequality
(*)
is due to

\displaystyle-Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)-g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)+g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\frac{L\nu(t)}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)+g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\frac{L}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))f^{\prime}\left(\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)+L\left\langle\nabla_{\tilde{q}}g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right),\tilde{q}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\leq}

\displaystyle-Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right),

where
\tilde{q}=(\tilde{q}_{i})_{i=1}^{N}
inequality
(*)
comes from Jensen Inequality and
g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)
is concave with respect to
\tilde{q}
.

Combining eqs. (
16
) and (
17
), we have

\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right\|\geq\frac{LC_{1}}{B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right)}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2L}\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|.

(18)

On the other hand,

\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right\|=\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\|\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\|,

while

\displaystyle\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}(\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t))+g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\frac{\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}+\frac{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\right)\left\|\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\langle\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\rangle\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\right\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right\|.

(19)

Since

\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|<\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{\frac{L}{4}}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|,

combining eqs. (
18
) and (
19
), we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right\|\geq

\displaystyle\frac{LC_{1}}{B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right)}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2L}\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{LC_{1}}{B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right)}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{\frac{7}{4}L}\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{LC_{1}}{B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right)}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{\frac{3}{4}L}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)\right\|.

Since
\bar{\partial}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
is parallel to
\bar{\partial}g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)
, we also have

\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|\geq\frac{LC_{1}}{B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right)}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{\frac{3}{4}L}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|.

Furthermore,

\left\|\bar{\partial}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|=\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|+\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|\leq\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{\frac{L}{4}}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|.

Thus, by Lemma
26
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\gamma}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\bar{\gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\backslash\backslash}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{LC_{1}}{2(B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right))}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{\frac{1}{2}L}\left\|\bar{\partial}\bar{\gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{LC_{1}}{2(B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right))}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{\frac{1}{2}L+1}\left\|\bar{\partial}\bar{\gamma}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|\frac{\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{LC_{1}}{2(B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right))\max\left\{1,\frac{4}{L}\right\}\Psi_{2}^{\prime}(\gamma_{0}-\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))}\frac{\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|}{\left\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|}.

Concluding
Case I.
and
Case II.
, for any
t\geq\max\{T_{1},T_{2}\}
, and
\Psi(x)=\max\{4\Psi_{1}(x),

\frac{2(B_{1}+Lg\left(\log\frac{1}{Ne^{-f(0)}}\right))\max\left\{1,\frac{4}{L}\right\}}{LC_{1}}\Psi_{2}(x)\}
, we have that

\Psi^{\prime}(\gamma_{0}-\bar{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\gamma}(t)}{\mathrm{d}t}\geq\frac{\left\|\bar{\partial}_{\perp}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right)\right\|}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}.

The proof is completed.
∎

Appendix D
Proof for the Discrete Case

We prove the result for AdaGrad and experiential loss, with the result for RMSProp and logistic loss follows exactly as the continuous case. We slightly change the order of four stages in the flow: First, in Section
D.1
, we prove that the conditioner has a limit with no zero entry; secondly, in Section
D.2
, we prove that the empirical loss converges to zero; then, in Section
D.3
, we construct a further smoothed approximate margin, and prove it has a lower bound; finally, in Section
D.4
, we prove that every limit point of AdaGrad is along some KKT point of optimization problem
(P^{A})
defined in Theorem
6
.

D.1
Convergence of conditioners

Before the proof, we give a formal definition of the learning rate bound
C(t)
in Assumption
2
: let
M
be the smooth constant in Assumption
2
. I. Then,
C(t)=\max\{\min_{i}\{\boldsymbol{h}^{A}_{i}(t)^{-1}\}/M,1,\frac{C^{2}_{1}}{2LNe^{-1}}\}
, where
C_{0}
will be clear below. By the monotony of
\boldsymbol{h}^{A}_{i}(t)
, apparently
C(t)
is non-decreasing. Now we can prove
\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))^{2}<\infty
.

Lemma 28
.

Suppose
\mathcal{L}
is
M
smooth with respect to
\boldsymbol{w}
. Then, for
\{\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))\}_{t=1}^{\infty}
updated by AdaGrad (eq. (
3
)),
\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))^{2}<\infty
.

Proof.

For any
t>t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))-\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t+1))

\displaystyle=\eta_{t}\langle\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t)),\boldsymbol{h}^{A}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))\rangle-\frac{M}{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{h}^{A}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\eta_{t}\frac{1}{2}\langle\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t)),\boldsymbol{h}^{A}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{\eta}\frac{1}{2}\langle\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t)),\boldsymbol{h}^{A}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))\rangle.

Thus, since
\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}a_{t}
share the same convergent behavior with
\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\frac{a_{t}}{\sum_{\tau=1}^{t}a_{\tau}}
(
a_{t}\geq 0
), by similar routine of Lemma
12
, the proof is completed.
∎

Therefore,
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}\overset{\triangle}{=}\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\boldsymbol{h}^{A}(t)
has no zero entry. We can then define a discrete version of adaptive gradient flow as

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}(t)=\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-1/2}\odot\boldsymbol{w}(t),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)=\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-1}\odot\boldsymbol{h}(t),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})=\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}),

and

\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v})=q_{i}(\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}),

which further leads to

\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)-\boldsymbol{v}(t)=-\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}_{ind}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),

(20)

and
\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)
decreases component-wisely to
\mathbf{1}_{p}
.

By Lemma
28
, for any
t\geq t_{0}
,
\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))\leq\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t_{0}))<N
. Therefore, there exists a positive real constant
C_{0}
only depending on
\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t_{0}))
, such that,
\|\boldsymbol{w}(t)\|\geq C_{0}
. Furthermore, since
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\geq\boldsymbol{h}(t_{0})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
,
\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|\geq C_{0}\max_{i}\{\boldsymbol{h}_{i}(t_{0})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}
. Define
C_{1}=C_{0}\max_{i}\{\boldsymbol{h}_{i}(t_{0})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\}
, which only depends on
\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t_{0}))
and
\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))

(t\leq t_{0})
.

Moreover, similar to approximate flow, there exists a time
t_{1}
, such that, for any time
t\geq t_{1}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\log\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-1}(t)\|\geq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}.

D.2
Convergence of Empirical Loss

Define function
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}
as the rate of
\log\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}}
to
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{\frac{L}{4}}
:

\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{\frac{L}{4}}}.

Then, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 29
.

For any
t\geq t_{1}
,
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t)
is non-decreasing.

Proof.

Since by Lemma
28
,
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
is non-increasing, if
\|\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|\leq\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|
, the proposition trivially holds. Therefore, we consider the case that
\|\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|>\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|
in the following proof.

The change of
\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|
can be calculated as

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\eta_{t}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle+\eta_{t}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}.

Let
A=-\eta_{t}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle
, and
B=\eta_{t}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}
. We estimate them separately.

As for
A
:

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=-\eta_{t}\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{t}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{t}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{t}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|\frac{\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}{\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{t}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}{\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle}.

As for
B
:

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\eta_{t}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{t}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{LNe^{-1}}{L\nu(t)}2\eta_{t}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{LNe^{-1}}{L\nu(t)C_{1}^{2}}2\eta^{2}_{t}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{t}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}{\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle}.

Therefore,

\|\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}\leq 4\eta_{t}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}{\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle}.

(21)

On the other hand, by Lemma
28
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t+1))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\langle\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t)),\eta_{t}\boldsymbol{h}^{A}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w}(t))\rangle

\displaystyle=\eta_{t}\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}.

(22)

Combining eqs. (
21
) and (
22
), we have

4\frac{1}{\nu(t)}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)))\geq\frac{L}{2}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}.

Since
\nu(t)\geq\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}
, we further have

\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)))\geq\frac{L}{8}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}.

By the convexity of
\log\log\frac{1}{x}
(when
x
is small) and
-\log x
,

\log\log\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{v}(t+1))}-\log\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\geq\frac{L}{4}(\log\|\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|-\log\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|).

The proof is completed.
∎

Remark 3
.

Actually, the convexity does not hold for
x\in[e^{-1},\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t_{1}))]
(if
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t_{1}))>e^{-1}
). However, we can instead define

\Phi_{0}(x)=\log\log\frac{1}{x}+\int_{0}^{x}\inf\{-\frac{1}{w\log\frac{1}{w}}:w\in[x,\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t_{1}))}]\}+\frac{1}{x\log\frac{1}{x}}dw.

Which satisfies for
x\in[0,\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t_{1}))]
,
\frac{1}{x\log\frac{1}{x}}\leq-\Phi^{\prime}_{0}(x)
,
\Phi_{0}(x)\leq\log\log x
, and
\Phi_{0}(x)
is convex. We can then replace
\log x
by
e^{-\Phi_{0}(x)}
and prove the above theorem in exactly the same way.0

With the relationship between
\|\boldsymbol{v}\|
and
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
, we can now prove that the empirical loss goes to zero.

Theorem 12
.

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=0
. Furthermore,
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\rho(t)=\infty
.

Proof.

By Lemma
32
, for any integer time
t\geq t_{0}

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t+1)-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)\leq-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t}\frac{L^{2}\nu(t)^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}\leq-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t}\frac{L^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)^{2}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)^{2}}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}.

Furthermore, since
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t)\geq\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})
, we have that

\left(\frac{\log(\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)})}{M_{1}^{L}\hat{\gamma}(t_{0})}\right)^{\frac{1}{L}}\geq\rho.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t+1)-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t}\frac{L^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)^{2}(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)})^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t}L^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)^{2}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})}{\log(\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)})}\right)^{\frac{8}{L}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t}L^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)^{2}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}\right)^{2-\frac{8}{L}}\left(\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})\right)^{\frac{8}{L}}.

Let
E_{0}=\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(t_{1}\right)^{2}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(t_{1}\right)}\right)^{2-8/L}
, then
\psi(x)=\min\{x^{2}(\log\frac{1}{x})^{2-\frac{8}{L}},E_{0}\}
. Apparently,
\psi(x)
is non-decreasing in
(0,\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1})]
. Therefore,
E(x)=\int_{x}^{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1})}\psi(s)ds
is convex with respect to
x
,
and

\displaystyle E(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t+1))-E(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))

\displaystyle\geq E^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t+1)-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t}L^{2}\left(\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})\right)^{\frac{8}{L}},

which further implies

E(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))-E(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t_{1}))\geq\sum_{\tau=t_{0}}^{t-1}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\tau}L^{2}\left(\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})\right)^{\frac{2}{L}}.

Since
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\tau=t_{0}}^{t-1}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\tau}L^{2}\left(\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})\right)^{\frac{8}{L}}=\infty
, we then have

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}E(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))=\infty,

and as a result,

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=0.

∎

D.3
Convergence of surrogate margin

As a preparation, define
B_{1}=\max\{\|\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v})\|:i\in[N],\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{B}(1)\}
, and
B_{2}=\max_{\{}\|\mathcal{H}\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v})\|:i\in[N],\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{B}(1)\}
.

Furthermore, we define
\lambda(x)=(\log\frac{1}{x})^{-1}
, and
\mu(x)=\frac{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t_{1}))}}{\log\frac{1}{x}}
. Since
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=0
, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 30
.

There exists a large enough time
t_{2}\geq t_{1}
, such that, for any
t\geq t_{2}
,

B_{1}^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\log^{7-\frac{8}{L}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}/\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})^{\frac{8}{L}}\leq\lambda(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))),

and,

\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})^{8-8/L}}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\log^{8-8/L}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\left(B_{1}^{2}+C_{1}^{-L}B_{2}\right)\leq\mu(t).

Proof.

The proposition is obvious since
\log^{i}(\frac{1}{x})=\mathbf{o}(x)
,
\forall i
as
x\rightarrow 0
.
∎

Then, we define a further surrogate margin
\hat{\gamma}
of the discrete case as following:

\hat{\gamma}(t):=\frac{e^{\Phi(\tilde{\mathcal{L}})}}{\rho^{L}},

where
\rho(t)
is defined as
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|
, and
\Phi(x)
is defined as

\Phi(x)=\log\log\frac{1}{x}+\int_{0}^{x}\left(-\sup\left\{\frac{1+2(1+\lambda(\tilde{w})/L)\mu(\tilde{w})}{\tilde{w}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{w}}}:\tilde{w}\in\left[w,\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(t_{2}\right)\right]\right\}+\frac{1}{w\log\frac{1}{w}}\right)dw.

The following properties hold for
\hat{\gamma}
.

Lemma 31
.

•

Let a series of
\boldsymbol{v}_{i}
satisfies
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\|=\infty
. Then,
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\hat{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}_{i})}{\tilde{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}_{i})}=1
;

•

If
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\leq\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t_{2}))
, then
\hat{\gamma}(t)<\tilde{\gamma}(t)\leq\bar{\gamma}(t)
.

Proof.

As beginning, we verify the existence of
\Phi
. Actually, when
x
is small enough,

\frac{1+2(1+\lambda(x)/L)\mu(x)}{x\log\frac{1}{x}}
decreases, and
\lim_{x\rightarrow 0}\frac{1+2(1+\lambda(x)/L)\mu(x)}{x\log\frac{1}{x}}=\infty
. Therefore, there exists a small enough
\varepsilon
, such that, for any
w<\varepsilon
,

\sup\left\{\frac{1+2(1+\lambda(\tilde{w})/L)\mu(\tilde{w})}{\tilde{w}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{w}}}:\tilde{w}\in\left[w,\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(t_{2}\right)\right]\right\}=\frac{1+2(1+\lambda(w)/L)\mu(w)}{w\log\frac{1}{w}},

which further leads to

\displaystyle-\sup\left\{\frac{1+2(1+\lambda(\tilde{w})/L)\mu(\tilde{w})}{\tilde{w}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{w}}}:\tilde{w}\in\left[w,\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(t_{2}\right)\right]\right\}+\frac{1}{w\log\frac{1}{w}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2(1+\lambda(w)/L)\mu(w)}{w\log\frac{1}{w}},

which is integrable as
w\rightarrow 0
. Concretely, for any
x<\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}\left(-\sup\left\{\frac{1+2(1+\lambda(\tilde{w})/L)\mu(\tilde{w})}{\tilde{w}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{w}}}:\tilde{w}\in\left[w,\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\left(t_{2}\right)\right]\right\}+\frac{1}{w\log\frac{1}{w}}\right)dw

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{x}-\frac{2(1+\lambda(w)/L)\mu(w)}{w\log\frac{1}{w}}dw

\displaystyle=-\log\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}(t_{1})}\left(\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{x}}+\frac{1}{2L\log^{2}\frac{1}{x}}\right).

Therefore, for a series
\{\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
satisfying
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{i}\|=\infty
,

\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\tilde{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}_{i})}{\hat{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{v}_{i})}=\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}e^{-\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\log^{2}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}_{i})}}\right)}=1.

Furthermore, if
x\leq\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t_{2}))
,

\Phi^{\prime}(x)\leq\frac{1}{w\log\frac{1}{w}}-\frac{1+2(1+\lambda(\tilde{w})/L)\mu(\tilde{w})}{\tilde{w}\log\frac{1}{\tilde{w}}}<0,

which proves that
\hat{\gamma}(t)<\tilde{\gamma}(t)
.

The proof is completed.
∎

To bound the norm of first and second derivatives of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
, we further need the following lemma.

The next lemma characterizes the behavior of surrogate margin
\hat{\gamma}(t)
.

Lemma 32
.

For positive integer time
t\geq t_{2}
,
\hat{\gamma}(t)\geq e^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{\gamma}(t_{2})
.

Proof.

For any time
t\geq t_{2}
,

\rho(t+1)^{2}-\rho(t)^{2}=(\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}-\rho(t)^{2})+(\rho(t+1)^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}).

We calculate two parts separately

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{t}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}+2L\eta_{t}\nu(t)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0.

On the other hand, since
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
is non-decreasing,

\rho(t+1)=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|\geq\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|.

\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}
can also be upper bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{t}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}+2L\eta_{t}\nu(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2L\eta_{t}\nu(t)\left(\frac{\eta_{t}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}}{2L\nu(t)}+1\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\leq}

\displaystyle 2L\eta_{t}\nu(t)\left(\frac{\lambda(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))}{L}+1\right),

where inequality (*) comes from the estimation of
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}
as follows: by the homogeneity of
\tilde{q}_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\|

\displaystyle=\left\|-\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v})}\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v})\right\|\overset{(**)}{\leq}B_{1}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L-1}

\displaystyle\overset{(***)}{\leq}B_{1}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})^{\frac{4}{L}}}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\log^{4-\frac{4}{L}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})},

where inequality
(**)
is due to
\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}
is
(L-1)
homogeneous, and inequality
(***)
holds by Lemma
29
.
On the other hand,
\nu(t)\geq\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}
. Combining the estimation of
\nu
and
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t))\|
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{t}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|^{2}}{2L\nu(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{B_{1}^{2}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})\log^{7-\frac{8}{L}}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})}}{L\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})^{\frac{8}{L}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))}{L}.

Similar to Lemma
28
, the decrease of
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})
can be calculated by second order Taylor Expansion:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t+1)-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=

\displaystyle-\eta_{t}\left\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\right\rangle

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}})^{T}\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)))(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}),

(23)

where
\xi\in(0,1)
.

By homogeneity of
\tilde{q}_{i}
, the norm of Hessian matrix
\|\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)))\|
can be bounded as

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)))\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-\tilde{q}_{i}}\left(\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}\partial^{s}\tilde{q}_{i}^{\top}-\mathcal{H}\tilde{q}_{i}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\leq}\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-\tilde{q}_{i}}\left(B_{1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{2L-2}+B_{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{L-2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi))\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{2L-2}\left(B_{1}^{2}+C_{1}^{-L}B_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})^{8-8/L}}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi))\log^{8-8/L}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi))}\left(B_{1}^{2}+C_{1}^{-L}B_{2}\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\eta_{t}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}})^{T}\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)))(\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{t}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}})\|^{2}\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}(t_{1})^{8-8/L}}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi))\log^{8-8/L}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi))}\left(B_{1}^{2}+C_{1}^{-L}B_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{t}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}})\|^{2}\mu(t).

(24)

Taking the estimation eq. (
24
) back to eq. (
23
), we have

\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t+1)\geq(1-\mu(t))\eta_{t}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}})\|^{2}

(25)

By multiplying
\frac{1+\lambda(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))/L}{(1-\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)))\nu(t)}
to both sides of eq. (
25
), we then have

\displaystyle\frac{1+\lambda(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))/L}{(1-\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)))\nu(t)}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t+1)-\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\eta_{t}\left(1+\frac{\lambda(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))}{L}\right)\frac{L^{2}\nu(t)}{\rho(t)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{L}{2}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{2}-\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}}.

Furthermore, since
-\Phi^{\prime}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))\geq\frac{1+\lambda(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))/L}{(1-\mu(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)))\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)/\lambda(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t))}
, by the convexity of
\Phi
and
-\log x
, we have that

\displaystyle L\left(\log\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}-\log\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\Phi(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t+1))-\Phi(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)))\geq 0.

Therefore,

\log\frac{\Phi(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t+1))}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{L}}-\log\frac{\Phi(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\geq 0.

Furthermore, since

\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{L}}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{L}}\geq\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\beta_{i}^{-L/2}(t)}{\beta_{i}^{-L/2}(t+1)},

we have

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}(t+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-\Phi(t+1)}}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{L}}\geq\frac{e^{-\Phi(t+1)}}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|^{L}}\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\beta_{i}^{-L/2}(t)}{\beta_{i}^{-L/2}(t+1)}

\displaystyle\geq\hat{\gamma}(t)\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\beta_{i}^{-L/2}(t)}{\beta_{i}^{-L/2}(t+1)}.

Thus, by induction,

\hat{\gamma}(t+1)\geq\hat{\gamma}(t)\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\beta_{i}^{-L/2}(t_{0})}{\beta_{i}^{-L/2}(t+1)}\geq e^{-\frac{1}{2}}\hat{\gamma}(t_{1}).

The proof is completed.
∎

Similar to the flow case, we can then prove the convergence of
\hat{\gamma}
.

Lemma 33
.

There exists a positive real
\hat{\gamma}_{\infty}
, such that

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\hat{\gamma}(t)=\hat{\gamma}_{\infty}.

Proof.

Since for any
t\geq t_{2}

\log\frac{\hat{\gamma}(t+1)}{\hat{\gamma}(t)}\geq\log\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t+1)},

we have that
\hat{\gamma}(t)\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}
monotonously increases. Furthermore, since
\hat{\gamma}(t)<\gamma(t)
is bounded, so does
\hat{\gamma}(t)\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}
. Therefore,
\hat{\gamma}(t)\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}
converges to a positive real. Since
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}=1
, the proof is completed.
∎

D.4
Verification of KKT point

Similar to the flow case, we have the following construction of
(\varepsilon,\delta)
KKT point. The proof is exactly the same as Lemma
5
, and we omit it here.

Lemma 34
.

Let
\lambda_{i}=q_{\min}^{1-2/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|\cdot e^{-f\left(q_{i}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(q_{i}\right)/\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|_{2}
. Then
\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)
is a
(\varepsilon,\delta)
KKT point of
(\tilde{P})
, where
\varepsilon
,
\delta
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\varepsilon

\displaystyle=C_{1}(1+\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}))

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle=C_{2}\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}}},

where
\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta})
is defined as
\langle\hat{v}(t),\widehat{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}}(t)\rangle
, and
C_{1},C_{2}
are positive real constants.

By Lemma
22
, we only need to prove that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta})}=1
. Furthermore, the estimation of
\cos(\boldsymbol{\tilde{\theta}})
can be given by the following lemma.

Lemma 35
.

For any
t_{3}>t_{4}\geq t_{2}
,

\sum_{\tau=t_{3}}^{t_{4}-1}\left(\tilde{\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta})}(\tau)^{-2}-1\right)\left(\log\frac{1}{\rho(t)}-\log\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau+1)\|}\right)\leq\frac{1}{L}\log\frac{\hat{\gamma}(t_{4})}{\hat{\gamma}(t_{3})}+\log\left(\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t_{3})}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t_{4})}\right).

Proof.

By Lemma
32
, for any
t\geq t_{0}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\log\frac{\hat{\gamma}(t+1)}{\hat{\gamma}(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\left(\Phi(t+1)-\Phi(t)\right)+\left(\log\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}-\log\frac{1}{\rho(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\log\frac{1}{\rho(t)}-\log\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}\right)\frac{\rho(t)^{2}}{L^{2}\nu(t)^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\log\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}-\log\frac{1}{\rho(t)}\right)-\log\left(\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)}{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}\right).

Furthermore, since
L\nu(t)=\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}}\right\rangle
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\log\frac{\hat{\gamma}(t+1)}{\hat{\gamma}(t)}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\log\frac{1}{\rho(t)}-\log\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}\right)\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta})^{-2}

\displaystyle+\left(\log\frac{1}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}-\log\frac{1}{\rho(t)}\right)-\log\left(\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)}{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)}\right).

The proof is completed.
∎

We still need a lemma to bound the change of the direction of
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}
.

Lemma 36
.

For any
t\geq t_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}}(t+1)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t+1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t)\right)+\left(\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}{\rho(t)}+1\right)\log\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}{\rho(t)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}{\rho(t)}+1\right)\log\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t+1)}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}.

Proof.

Since triangular inequality,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}}(t+1)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)-\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)-\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)-\frac{1}{\rho(t)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|.

Let

\displaystyle A=\left\|\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)-\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|;

\displaystyle B=\left\|\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)-\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|;

\displaystyle C=\left\|\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)-\frac{1}{\rho(t)}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\|.

Then,

\displaystyle A=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)-\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{1}{\rho(t+1)}\|\eta_{t}\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)\odot\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|=\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\eta_{t}}{\rho(t+1)}\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\eta_{t}\nu(t)}{\hat{\gamma}(t_{2})\rho(t+1)\rho(t)}\leq\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\rho(t+1)^{2}-\rho(t)^{2}}{\rho(t+1)\rho(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\rho(t+1)^{2}-\rho(t)^{2}}{\rho(t+1)^{2}}\frac{\rho(t+1)}{\rho(t)}\leq\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\rho(t+1)}{\rho(t)}\log\frac{\rho(t+1)}{\rho(t)},

where eq.
(*)
can be derived in the same way as Lemma
6
;

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{p}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t))^{2}\frac{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}{\rho(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t+1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t);

and

\displaystyle C=1-\frac{\rho(t)}{\rho(t+1)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)\odot\boldsymbol{v}}(t+1)-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t+1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t)\right)+\left(\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\rho(t+1)}{\rho(t)}+1\right)\log\frac{\rho(t+1)}{\rho(t)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1)\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t+1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-1}(t)\right)+\left(\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}{\rho(t)}+1\right)\log\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}{\rho(t)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t+1)\|}{\rho(t)}+1\right)\log\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t+1)}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}

∎

We then prove that
\sum_{\tau=t_{2}}^{\infty}\log\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau+1)\|}{\rho(\tau)}=\infty
.

Lemma 37
.

The sum of
\log\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau+1)\|}{\rho(\tau)}
diverges, that is,
\sum_{\tau=t_{2}}^{\infty}\log\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau+1)\|}{\rho(\tau)}=\infty
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau=t}^{\infty}\log\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau+1)\|}{\rho(\tau)}\geq\sum_{\tau=t}^{\infty}\log\frac{\rho(\tau+1)}{\rho(\tau)}-\log\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}.

The proof is completed since
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\rho(t)=\infty
and
\log\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(t)}
is bounded.
∎

Now we can prove the following lemma.

Lemma 38
.

Let
\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}
be any limit point of
\{\boldsymbol{v}(t)\}_{t=1}^{\infty}
. Then
\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}
is a KKT point of optimization problem
(P)
.

Proof.

Let
t^{1}
be any integer time larger than
t_{2}
. We construct a sequence
\{t^{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
by iteration. Suppose
t^{1},\cdots,t^{k-1}
have been constructed. Let
s^{k}>t^{k-1}
be a large enough time which satisfies

\displaystyle\log\Pi_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{i}(s^{k})}\leq\frac{1}{k^{3}},

\displaystyle\|\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(s^{k})-\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}\|\leq\frac{1}{k},

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{\infty}}{\hat{\gamma}(s^{k})}\right)\leq\frac{1}{k^{3}}

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-1}(s^{k})\|\leq 1+\frac{1}{k-1}.

Then let
(s^{k})^{\prime}
(guaranteed by Lemma
37
) be the first time greater than
s^{k}
that
\sum_{\tau=s^{k}}^{(s^{k})^{\prime}-1}\log\frac{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\tau)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(\tau+1)\|}{\rho(\tau)}\geq\frac{1}{k}
. By Lemma
35
, there exists a time
t^{k}\in[s^{k},(s^{k})^{\prime}-1]
, such that
\tilde{\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta})}^{-2}-1\leq\frac{1}{k^{2}}
.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\|\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t^{k})-\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t^{k})\odot\boldsymbol{v}}(t^{k})-\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}\|^{2}+\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t^{k})\odot\boldsymbol{v}}(t^{k})-\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t^{k})\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=2\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t^{k})\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k})}-\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}\|^{2}+2\left(2-2\left\langle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t^{k})\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k})},\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t^{k})\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t^{k})\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k})}-\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}\|^{2}+O\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(t^{k})\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t^{k})}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\beta}(s^{k})\odot\boldsymbol{v}(s^{k})}\|^{2}+O\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(1)\frac{1}{k}+O(e^{\frac{1}{k}})\frac{1}{k}\rightarrow 0.

The proof is completed.

∎

Therefore, similar to the gradient flow case, we then have the following theorem.

Theorem 13
.

Let
\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}
be any limit point of
\{\hat{\boldsymbol{w}}(t)\}
. Then
\bar{\boldsymbol{w}}
is along the direction of a KKT point of the following optimization problem.

\displaystyle\text{Minimize }\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{w}\|

\displaystyle\text{Subject to: }q_{i}(w)\geq 1.

Appendix E
Proof of Multi-class Classification with Logistic Loss

In this section, we prove the result for multi-class classification with logistic loss mentioned in Remark
1
. Concretely, the dataset for this case can be represented as
\{(\boldsymbol{x}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}
, where
y_{i}\in[C]
represents the class
\boldsymbol{x}_{i}
belongs to. Unlike the binary classification case, neural network
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
outputs a
C
-dimension vector as scores for
C
classes, and we use
\boldsymbol{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}
as the
j
-th component of
\boldsymbol{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})
. The empirical loss can then be represented as

\mathcal{L}(\boldsymbol{w})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}-\log\frac{e^{\boldsymbol{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{C}e^{\boldsymbol{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}}}.

(26)

For AdaGrad, RMSProp, and Adam (w/m), limit
\boldsymbol{h}^{A}_{\infty}
,
\boldsymbol{h}^{R}_{\infty}
,
\boldsymbol{h}^{M}_{\infty}
remains non-zero, and we can then define
\boldsymbol{v}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
, and
\boldsymbol{\beta}
the same as Theorems
6
and
7
(we use
\boldsymbol{v}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
to represent all cases), and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})=\boldsymbol{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\odot\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})
.

We can then define margins in the multi-class classification similarly as the binary case: surrogate norm and margin are defined exactly the same as the binary case; define
\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v})=\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}-\max_{j\neq y_{i}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}
and normalized margin can be still defined as
\frac{\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{L}}
. The corresponding convergent direction for adaptive gradient flow under multi-class setting can then be characterized by the following theorem:

Theorem 14
.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}
satisfy an adaptive gradient flow
\mathcal{F}
which satisfies Assumption
1
. Let
\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}
be any limit point of
\{\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
(where
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)=\frac{\boldsymbol{v}(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}
is normalized parameter). Then
\bar{\boldsymbol{v}}
is along the direction of a KKT point of the following
L^{2}
max-margin problem
(P)
:

\displaystyle\min\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\text{subject to }\tilde{q}_{i}(\boldsymbol{v})\geq 1,\forall i\in[N].

Proof of Theorem
14
differs from that of Theorem
2
only by Lemma
18
, Lemma
19
, and the construction of
\lambda_{i}
in Lemma
21
. We show modifications respectively.

First of all, we show normalized margin
\gamma
and surrogate margin
\tilde{\gamma}
converge to the same limit:

Lemma 39
.

Let a function
\boldsymbol{v}(t)
obey an adaptive gradient flow
\mathcal{F}
which satisfies Assumption
1
, with loss
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
and component learning rate
\boldsymbol{\beta}(t)
. Then we have
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\rho(t)}{\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|}=1
. Furthermore, if further
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(t)=\infty
, we have
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\gamma(t)}{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}=1
.

Proof.

By definition of empirical loss (eq. (
26
)),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}-\log\frac{e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{C}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}-\log\frac{1}{1+\sum_{j\neq y_{i}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}}.

Let
\tilde{q}_{i}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{v})=\log\sum_{j\neq y_{i}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}
. By exact the same routine of Lemma
18
, we have

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\gamma(t)}{\frac{\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\rho^{L}}}=1.

(27)

On the other hand,

-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})\leq\log\sum_{j\neq y_{i}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}\leq-\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})+\log N,

which leads to

\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\tilde{\gamma}(t)}{\frac{\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v})}{\rho^{L}}}=1.

(28)

Combining eqs. (
27
) and (
28
), the proof is completed.

∎

Secondly, we calculate derivative of surrogate norm
\rho
under multi-class classification setting.

Lemma 40
.

The derivative of
\rho^{2}
is as follows:

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho(t)^{2}}{\mathrm{d}t}=L\nu(t)+\left\langle\boldsymbol{v}(t),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(t)\odot\frac{\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\mathrm{d}t}(t)\odot\boldsymbol{v}(t)\right\rangle,

where
\nu(t)
is defined as

\nu(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\sum_{j\neq y_{n}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j})}{1+\sum_{j\neq y_{n}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}}.

Furthermore, we have that
\nu(t)>\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))
.

Proof.

We only need to show

-\langle\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),\boldsymbol{v}(t)\rangle=L\nu(t),

(29)

and

\nu(t)>\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)),

(30)

while other parts of the proof follows exact the same as Lemma
19
.

By chain rule,

\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\sum_{j\neq y_{i}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}\partial^{s}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}})}{1+\sum_{j\neq y_{i}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}},

while by homogeneity of
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}
,

\langle\partial^{s}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}),\boldsymbol{v}\rangle=L(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}),

which completes the proof of eq. (
29
).

As for eq. (
30
),

\displaystyle\nu(t)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-f(\tilde{q}_{i}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))}f^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))\frac{\sum_{j\neq y_{i}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j})}{\sum_{j\neq y_{i}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}e^{-f(\tilde{q}_{i}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))}f^{\prime}(\tilde{q}_{i}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)))\tilde{q}_{i}^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{g\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}{g^{\prime}\left(\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}\right)}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)).

The proof is completed.

∎

Finally, we provide construction of
\lambda_{i}
similar to Lemma
21
. The proof follows the same routine as Lemma
21
and we omit it here.

Lemma 41
.

Let
\boldsymbol{v}
obey adaptive gradient flow
\mathcal{F}
with empirical loss
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
satisfying Assumption
1
. Let time
t_{1}
be constructed as Lemma
2
. Then, define coefficients in Definition
2
as

\lambda_{i,j}(t)=\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{1-2/L}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|\cdot\frac{1}{\|\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))\|_{2}}\frac{1+e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}}{\sum_{j\neq y_{i}}e^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{j}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}(\boldsymbol{v},\boldsymbol{x}_{i})_{y_{i}}}},

(where

i\in[N]
,
j\in[C]/\{i\}
). Then, for any time
t\geq t_{1}
,
\tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)=\tilde{q}_{\min}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))^{-\frac{1}{L}}\boldsymbol{v}(t)
is an
(\varepsilon(t),\delta(t))
KKT point of
(\tilde{P})
, where
\varepsilon(t)
,
\delta(t)
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\varepsilon(t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1-\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t)))

\displaystyle\delta(t)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\log\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}}\right),

where
\cos(\boldsymbol{\theta}(t))
is defined as inner product of
\hat{\boldsymbol{v}}(t)
and
-\widehat{\partial^{s}\tilde{\mathcal{L}}(\boldsymbol{v}(t))}
.

Appendix F
Experiment Details

In this section, we provide detailed explanation of experiments showed in Section
6

4
4

4

https://github.com/bhwangfy/ICML-2021-Adaptive-Bias
. This section is divided into two parts according to Section
6
: in Section
F.1
, we provide details of structure of neural network we use and hyper-parameters. We also further plot two additional experiments of Adam and SGD to show the influence of momentum; in Section
F.3
, we show construction of dataset in Section
6.2
and choose of hyper-parameters. We also show how direction of
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
influence convergent direction of parameters.

F.1
Experiment on MNIST

F.1.1
Construction of Neural Network and Choice of Hyper-parameters

We use the
4
-layer convolutional neural network adopted by
(
Madry et al. 2018
)
as our model to conduct multi-class classification on MNIST
(
LeCun 1998
)
. Concretely, this convolutional neural network can be expressed in order as convolutional layer with
32
channel and filter size
5\times 5
, max-pool layer with kernel size
2
and stride
2
,
convolutional layer with
64
channel and filter size
3\times 3
, max-pool with kernel size
2
, fully connected layer with width
1024
, and fully connected layer with width
10
. In order to guarantee this neural network is homogeneous, we further set bias in all layers to be zero. We use default method in Pytorch to initialize the neural network.

As for hyper-parameters, we set learning rate of AdaGrad to be the default value in Pytorch; while for RMSProp, we set learning rate and decay parameter
b
as
0.001
and
0.9
, which is suggested by
(
Hinton et al. 2012
)
and used as a default value in Tensorflow; for Adam, we set the learning rate to
0.0001
as default value in Pytorch, and
b
to be the same as RMSProp.

F.1.2
Influence of Momentum

We plot convergent behaviors for SGDm and Adam in this section. Figure
4
shows that adding momentum term will NOT keep normalized margin from lower bounded, which indicates our theory might be extended to gradient based optimization methods with momentum. Specifically, for SGD, we use learning rate
0.1
and momentum parameter
0.9
; for Adam, we use the same setting as Adam (w/m) with momentum parameter
0.9
.

(b)

Test Accuracy

(c)

(d)

Normalized Margin

:
Observation of convergent behavior after adding momentum for SGD and Adam. One can observe that loss still converge to zero, and margin will keep lower bounded.

Normalized margin

Figure 5
:
Observation of generalization behavior of RMSProp with different
\varepsilon
. One can observe that larger
\varepsilon
leads to larger margin and smaller generalization error.

F.2
Influence of
\varepsilon

We compare the generalization behaviors of RMSProp with different
\varepsilon
selected in Figure
5
. It is observed that as
\varepsilon
decreases, normalized margin gets smaller and the generalization error gets larger, which indicates the importance of
\varepsilon
on the generalization behavior. When
\varepsilon
is completed removed (i.e., is set to
0
), the training does not converge. Therefore, we do not include the results for
\varepsilon=0
here.

F.3
Experiment on Two Layer MLP

F.3.1
Dataset Construction and Choice of Hyper-parameters

As mentioned in Section
6.2
, we use a two layer MLP
\Phi
with leaky ReLU activation
\sigma
defined as
\Phi(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{w},v)=v\sigma(\langle\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{x}\rangle)
, where
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\boldsymbol{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
v\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma(t)
is the Leaky ReLU activation function, i.e.,
\sigma(t)=t
for
t\geq 0
and
\sigma(t)=\frac{t}{2}
for
t<0
. We construct binary classification dataset
S
as
\{(\boldsymbol{x}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{100}
as follows:

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{x}_{i},y_{i})=((\cos(0.5),\sin{0.5})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i},1),\text{ }i\in\{1,2,\cdots,50\};

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{x}_{i},y_{i})=((-\cos(0.5),-\sin{0.5})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i},-1),\text{ }i\in\{51,52,\cdots,100\},

where
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{i}
(
i=1,2,3,\cdots,100
) are random variables sampled uniformly and i.i.d. from
[-0.6,0.6]\times[-0.6,0.6]
. We visualize the dataset in Figure
6(a)
.

(a)

Visualizing Constructed Dataset

(b)

Margin varies across runs

(c)

Training accuracy

(d)

Direction of
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in AdaGrad

(e)

Direction of
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in RMSProp

(f)

Direction of
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in Adam

Figure 6
:
Experiment on two layer MLP. In (a), we visualize constructed dataset as vectors, with green vectors standing for data with label
-1
, and red stands for data with label
1
. In (b), we plot convergent margin of different optimizers across runs. In (c), averaged training accuracy across runs is shown, and one can observe all training accuracy achieves
100\%
. (d)-(e) respectively picture
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in AdaGrad (red vectors), RMSProp (blue vectors) and Adam (black vectors) across runs. While direction of
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in AdaGrad varies across runs, RMSProp and Adam stay the same and coincide with isotropic direction (green vector)
(\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}})

We then run SGD, AdaGrad, RMSProp (and Adam (w/m)) respectively with learning rates
\eta=0.1
, while Weight-decay hyper-parameter
b
is set to be

0.9
. For each round, we train the model for
5000
epochs to ensure that training accuracy achieves
100\%
(see Figure
6(c)
for details); while for each optimizer, we conduct
100
rounds of experiments with random initialization, Convergent directions of square root of inverse conditioners
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
are plotted in Figures
6(d)
,
6(e)
, and
6(f)
. Since
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
occurs in optimization target in
(P^{A})
, different direction of
\boldsymbol{h}_{\infty}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
may lead to different convergent direction of parameters, which further indicates convergent direction of parameters in AdaGrad can be vulnerable to random initialization.
